NIGHTLY FRAUD RISK SCORING POLICY MANUAL
Revision: 2025-06-20 | Classification: INTERNAL USE ONLY
Owner: Card Risk Engineering | On-call: fraud-ops-nightly@internal

Document Control
----------------
This manual governs the overnight card-ledger verification pipeline, feature
materialization job, and logistic scoring worker deployed on the ops batch host.
Changes require sign-off from Card Risk Engineering and a same-day ops bulletin.
Prior revisions are archived in the internal wiki under Fraud/NightlyWorker.

Purpose
-------
Each night the card processor delivers signed ledger CSV batches. Before any
account features or model scores are written, the worker must prove ledger
provenance via an OpenSSL HMAC-SHA256 chain. Verified batches feed a SQLite
feature store; queued scoring jobs consume those features and emit signed
predictions back into the operations database at /var/lib/ops/ops.db.

On-Call Responsibilities
------------------------
Primary: confirm batch arrival, run verify-ledger, monitor scoring_jobs queue.
Secondary: page treasury if batch identifiers disagree with the intake manifest.
During an open Sev-2, do not hand-edit ledger_verifications, feature_store, or
scoring_predictions — re-run worker subcommands after upstream data is corrected.
All timestamps in worker output and database columns use UTC ISO-8601 ending in Z.

Nightly Workflow
----------------
1. Treasury publishes the batch manifest (batch_id, row count, file path).
2. On-call runs verify-ledger against the manifest batch_id and ledger path.
3. After chain_ok=1, run populate-features for that batch_id.
4. Drain queued scoring_jobs rows whose batch_id matches a verified batch.
5. Attach worker stdout captures to the nightly workbook ticket.

Batch Inventory and File Naming
-------------------------------
Ledger files live under /app/data/ledger/. Production naming convention is
batch_<nnn>.csv (e.g. batch_001.csv for BATCH-001). Each file contains columns:
batch_id, sequence, pan_hash, amount_cents, merchant_code, country, hmac.
The pending production batch for 2025-06-20 is BATCH-001 at
/app/data/ledger/batch_001.csv with job JOB-20250620-001 queued in scoring_jobs.

Database Inventory
------------------
/var/lib/ops/ops.db holds four worker-managed tables:
  ledger_verifications — batch_id, row_count, chain_ok, verified_at
  feature_store — per-account aggregates keyed by (batch_id, account_id)
  scoring_jobs — job queue with batch_id reference and status lifecycle
  scoring_predictions — signed risk scores keyed by (job_id, account_id)
Shared secrets: /app/config/hmac_secret.txt (ledger chain),
/app/config/predict_secret.txt (prediction signing). Trim trailing whitespace
when reading either secret file.

Merchant Category Reference
---------------------------
Merchant codes in ledger CSVs map to MCC groups for analyst triage only; the
worker passes merchant_code through verbatim for HMAC payloads.
  M010-M099 — retail and grocery
  M100-M199 — travel and entertainment
  M800-M899 — high-risk jurisdictions (elevated manual review rate)
The worker does not re-derive MCC groups during feature aggregation.

Regional Authorization Notes
----------------------------
Country codes are ISO-style two-letter values from the processor feed. Feature
aggregation treats only US as domestic; all other country codes count as
foreign for foreign_ratio. This matches treasury's cross-border reporting.

Scoring Job Queue
-----------------
Jobs are inserted by the scheduler with status queued. run-scoring processes a
single job id, loads the job's batch_id, reads feature_store rows for that
batch, writes scoring_predictions, then marks the job completed with
completed_at. Jobs must reference a batch that already has chain_ok=1.

Incident Response
-----------------
Sev-2: verify-ledger fails twice on a production batch — page Card Risk on-call.
Sev-3: populate-features row counts disagree with manifest — hold scoring queue.
Capture stdout from each subcommand in the incident ticket. Do not disable HMAC
verification as a workaround.

Glossary
--------
chain_ok — ledger_verifications flag; 1 means HMAC chain validated.
genesis row — sequence 0 anchor with pan_hash GENESIS anchoring the HMAC chain.
pan_hash — pseudonymous account identifier aggregated into feature_store.account_id.

Ledger Provenance Requirements
------------------------------
Card-ledger batches require a valid HMAC-SHA256 chain before enrichment or
scoring. Read the shared secret from /app/config/hmac_secret.txt and trim
trailing whitespace.

Row zero anchors the chain: compute HMAC-SHA256(secret, "GENESIS|<batch_id>").
Each later row signs
"<prev_hmac>|<pan_hash>|<amount_cents>|<merchant_code>|<country>" and stores
the lowercase hex digest in the hmac column.

The verify-ledger --batch argument must match the batch_id column on every CSV
row; genesis anchoring must use that --batch value, not an inferred value from
the file. Sequences must begin at zero and increase by one through N-1 without
gaps. Row zero must be the genesis anchor with pan_hash GENESIS.

On chain failure exit non-zero, write no ledger_verifications row, and do not
overwrite an existing row for that batch_id that already has chain_ok=1. On
success insert or replace a ledger_verifications row with row_count (every CSV
row including genesis), chain_ok=1, and verified_at as UTC ISO-8601 ending in Z.

Feature Store Requirements
--------------------------
After verification succeeds, aggregate non-genesis rows by pan_hash into table
feature_store (batch_id, account_id, tx_count, total_amount, avg_amount,
foreign_ratio, max_amount, populated_at). Skip any row where sequence is zero
or pan_hash is GENESIS. avg_amount equals total_amount divided by tx_count,
stored rounded to six decimal places before INSERT. foreign_ratio counts rows
whose country is not US divided by tx_count, also stored rounded to six decimal
places before INSERT. populated_at uses UTC ISO-8601 timestamps ending in Z.
Re-running populate-features for the same batch must replace existing rows (no
duplicate account rows). populate-features must refuse unverified batches and
must reject ledgers whose batch_id column does not match --batch on every row.

CLI Contract
------------
Build /app/risk_worker via Makefile with .c/.h sources directly under /app/.
Subcommands and stdout (exactly one line each on success):
  verify-ledger --batch <id> [--ledger <path>]
    -> VERIFY batch=<id> rows=<n> chain=OK  (<n> includes genesis row)
  populate-features --batch <id> [--ledger <path>]
    -> FEATURES batch=<id> accounts=<n>
  run-scoring --job <id>
    -> SCORE job=<id> accounts=<n> status=completed
When --ledger is omitted, populate-features defaults to
/app/data/ledger/batch_001.csv.

Model Calibration
-----------------
Overnight logistic regression for production nightly scoring:
  z = intercept + w_tx*tx_count + w_amt*total_amount + w_fr*foreign_ratio
      + w_max*max_amount
  risk_score = 1 / (1 + exp(-z)), rounded to six decimals before storage.

Approved coefficients: intercept -2.5; tx_count 0.15; total_amount 1e-05;
foreign_ratio 1.8; max_amount 5e-05.

Prediction Signing
------------------
Persist prediction_hmac as HMAC-SHA256(predict_secret, message) where
predict_secret comes from /app/config/predict_secret.txt (trim trailing
whitespace) and message is "<job_id>|<account_id>|<risk_score>" with six
fractional digits. Write signed_at in UTC ISO-8601. When all accounts for a
job are scored, set scoring_jobs.status to completed and write completed_at in
UTC ISO-8601.

Change Log
----------
2025-06-20 — Initial worker contract for BATCH-001 nightly replay.
2025-06-18 — Added foreign_ratio domestic rule (US-only domestic).
2025-06-15 — Migrated ledger HMAC from SHA-1 legacy to HMAC-SHA256.

Appendix D — Extended Data Dictionary
-------------------------------------

ledger_verifications.batch_id: Primary key; matches verify-ledger --batch.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing ledger_verifications.batch_id, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

ledger_verifications.row_count: Total CSV rows including genesis anchor.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing ledger_verifications.row_count, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

ledger_verifications.chain_ok: 1 when HMAC chain validated successfully.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing ledger_verifications.chain_ok, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

ledger_verifications.verified_at: UTC ISO-8601 timestamp ending in Z.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing ledger_verifications.verified_at, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

feature_store.account_id: pan_hash from ledger rows excluding genesis.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing feature_store.account_id, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

feature_store.tx_count: Count of ledger rows aggregated for the account.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing feature_store.tx_count, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

feature_store.total_amount: Sum of amount_cents for the account.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing feature_store.total_amount, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

feature_store.avg_amount: total_amount / tx_count rounded to six decimals.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing feature_store.avg_amount, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

feature_store.foreign_ratio: Non-US row fraction rounded to six decimals.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing feature_store.foreign_ratio, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

feature_store.max_amount: Maximum amount_cents seen for the account.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing feature_store.max_amount, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

feature_store.populated_at: UTC ISO-8601 timestamp ending in Z.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing feature_store.populated_at, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

scoring_jobs.job_id: Queue identifier consumed by run-scoring --job.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing scoring_jobs.job_id, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

scoring_jobs.batch_id: Must reference a verified batch before scoring.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing scoring_jobs.batch_id, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

scoring_jobs.status: Lifecycle: queued, running, completed, failed.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing scoring_jobs.status, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

scoring_predictions.risk_score: Sigmoid output rounded to six decimals.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing scoring_predictions.risk_score, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

scoring_predictions.prediction_hmac: HMAC-SHA256 over job|account|score.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing scoring_predictions.prediction_hmac, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

scoring_predictions.signed_at: UTC ISO-8601 timestamp ending in Z.
  Analyst tip: when reviewing scoring_predictions.signed_at, cross-check the nightly workbook entry and attach the worker stdout line that produced the value.

Note G-001: OpenSSL 3 HMAC-SHA256 on Bookworm
  Production worker links libssl/libcrypto and implements HMAC-SHA256 for ledger and prediction signing. When upgrading compiler images, rerun oracle verification on BATCH-001 before promoting the image.
  SQLite writes target /var/lib/ops/ops.db with foreign keys enabled. Use INSERT OR REPLACE for idempotent feature_store rows per batch.

Appendix H — Treasury Reconciliation FAQ
----------------------------------------

Q1: Why does treasury publish batch_id separately from the CSV filename?
A1: The manifest batch_id is authoritative for verify-ledger --batch even when the file name follows batch_<nnn>.csv convention.

Q2: How are genesis rows counted in row_count?
A2: row_count includes the genesis anchor row (sequence zero).

Q3: What domestic country code does feature aggregation use?
A3: Only US counts as domestic for foreign_ratio.

Q4: Where are model coefficients documented?
A4: See Model Calibration in the worker contract section of this manual.

Q5: Why does treasury publish batch_id separately from the CSV filename?
A5: The manifest batch_id is authoritative for verify-ledger --batch even when the file name follows batch_<nnn>.csv convention.

Q6: How are genesis rows counted in row_count?
A6: row_count includes the genesis anchor row (sequence zero).

Q7: What domestic country code does feature aggregation use?
A7: Only US counts as domestic for foreign_ratio.

Q8: Where are model coefficients documented?
A8: See Model Calibration in the worker contract section of this manual.

Q9: Why does treasury publish batch_id separately from the CSV filename?
A9: The manifest batch_id is authoritative for verify-ledger --batch even when the file name follows batch_<nnn>.csv convention.

Q10: How are genesis rows counted in row_count?
A10: row_count includes the genesis anchor row (sequence zero).

Q11: What domestic country code does feature aggregation use?
A11: Only US counts as domestic for foreign_ratio.

Q12: Where are model coefficients documented?
A12: See Model Calibration in the worker contract section of this manual.

A13: The manifest batch_id is authoritative for verify-ledger --batch even when the file name follows batch_<nnn>.csv convention.

A15: Only US counts as domestic for foreign_ratio.

Q19: What domestic country code does feature aggregation use?
A19: Only US counts as domestic for foreign_ratio.

Q20: Where are model coefficients documented?
A20: See Model Calibration in the worker contract section of this manual.

Q21: Why does treasury publish batch_id separately from the CSV filename?
A21: The manifest batch_id is authoritative for verify-ledger --batch even when the file name follows batch_<nnn>.csv convention.
